## Work-level decisions

<a id="shelf-8c55a0f6d20a"></a>
### The Lifetime Learner's Guide to Reading and Learning — Gary Hoover

Status: **excluded**. Sources: 258.

H3: procedural reading/study guide centered on selecting, extracting, retaining and connecting information, supplemented by a recommended-book list. Main purpose is a method of practice, not sustained explanation of discoveries. Evidence: direct reader report https://www.jermainebrown.org/books/the-lifetime-learners-guide-to-reading-and-learning ; publication verified https://openlibrary.org/authors/OL8653745A/Gary_Hoover . Eligibility confidence moderate because full text was not available. Confidence: moderate. No score assigned.

<a id="shelf-2a9e6cf4a2ad"></a>
### Learning How to Learn — Barbara Oakley

Status: **excluded**. Sources: 258.

H3: procedural study-skills training for students, with application questions and exercises. Its explanatory neuroscience supports skill acquisition. https://www.penguinrandomhouse.com/books/563935/learning-how-to-learn-by-barbara-oakley-phd-and-terrence-sejnowski-phd-with-alistair-mcconville/ Confidence: moderate. No score assigned.

<a id="shelf-b5e5cfd93187"></a>
### Mindshift — Barbara Oakley

Status: **recommended**. Sources: 258.

General motivational and explanatory account of adult learning and career change, with study tips but no primary specialist technical curriculum. https://www.penguinrandomhouse.com/books/530917/mindshift-by-barbara-oakley-phd/9781101982853/ verifies 2017 and international case-based structure. Selected success stories and neuroscience analogies do not prove that the strategies cause comparable outcomes for everyone, lowering E/M. D=6 low confidence from description and attributed review: contrasting retraining paths add some constraints, encouragement and personal success detail have lower yield. Self-help lowers fit but is not an extra exclusion rule; no established V3 because learning heuristics remain. No thematic or historical-period deduction.

Eligibility: nonfiction explanatory work, with no established hard exclusion or serious veto in the inspected source report and available bibliographic evidence. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-c69f39a02a87"></a>
### An Incomplete Education — Judy Jones and William Wilson

Status: **recommended**. Sources: 258.

Broad explanatory cultural compendium rather than a specialist technical reference or curriculum. https://www.penguinrandomhouse.com/books/88462/an-incomplete-education-by-judy-jones-and-william-wilson/ identifies coauthors and revised scope; https://search.worldcat.org/title/1153305331 confirms original 1987. General reference format alone is not the technical-reference exclusion. D=6.5 low confidence from description: conceptual summaries and comparisons add learning alongside facts, but disconnected cultural catch-up has limited mechanistic and strategic value. No single listed negative theme predominates, T11/T12=0; broad cross-period scope H13=0. Later editions' topical claims not treated as current 2026 facts.

Eligibility: nonfiction explanatory work, with no established hard exclusion or serious veto in the inspected source report and available bibliographic evidence. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-23888634af7c"></a>
### The Intellectual Life of the British Working Classes — Jonathan Rose

Status: **recommended**. Sources: 258.

Uses memoirs, library records and surveys to explain self-education and access to cultural resources, not merely isolated lives. https://yalebooks.yale.edu/book/9780300259827/the-intellectual-life-of-the-british-working-classes/ establishes evidence base and original 2001 recognition; later editions do not reset age. Central preindustrial-to-mid-twentieth-century Britain, approximate endpoint 1945, low confidence. T11 national social history; T12=0 because education rather than political mobilization dominates. D=7.5 low-confidence estimate from description and review extracts: contrasting reading institutions add learning, while lengthy local detail may have lower yield. No whole-text density audit.

Eligibility: nonfiction explanatory work, with no established hard exclusion or serious veto in the inspected source report and available bibliographic evidence. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

Status: **recommended**. Sources: 258.

Research synthesis explaining bounded rationality through computational models, experiments and contrasting environments; technically demanding but primary value is explanation of decision mechanisms rather than training in formal methods. https://www.oup.com.au/books/others/9780195143812-simple-heuristics-that-make-us-smart supplies chapter structure; https://obnb.uk/p12590901-simple-heuristics-that-make-us-smart establishes original 1999. D=8 low-confidence contents/description estimate: recognition, memory, categorization and social decisions add distinct constraints; comparisons do not establish heuristics as universally superior. Cognition/modeling is central, finance auxiliary; no thematic deductions or single historical endpoint. https://openlibrary.org/books/OL27087635M/Simple_heuristics_that_make_us_smart documents references 367–396 of 416 numbered pages.

### Lone Star: A History of Texas — T. R. Fehrenbach

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 258.

Counterfactual assessment retains a serious interpretive flaw. https://academic.oup.com/jah/article-abstract/107/2/447/5907752 identifies an overly simple Anglo-heroic exceptionalist narrative; https://www.wtamu.edu/_files/docs/2019%20PPHR%20Book.pdf discusses its persistent frontier-centered frame. https://www.texasobserver.org/fehrenbachs-texas/ provides representative argument and contrasting readers, including praise for integrating different experiences. V2 moderate: national identity and violent conquest organize an asymmetric account; not a veto merely for discussing violence or quoting historical slurs. D=6.5 low-confidence review estimate: substantial historical detail, reduced explanatory balance. T11 US regional history, T12 conquest and political power. H13=0 broad cross-period account.

Veto V2; confidence moderate. The central exceptionalist account privileges Anglo frontier conquest as the defining explanation of Texas, with asymmetric treatment of competing experiences. Specialist historiographic criticism and representative argument support ideological capture; this is not a veto for its subject or historical quotations. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-6ada589756e7"></a>
### A History of Money and Banking in the United States — Murray N. Rothbard, edited by Joseph T. Salerno

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 258.

Counterfactual V1 assessment of a historical essay collection, not technical instruction. https://www.si.edu/object/history-money-and-banking-united-states-colonial-era-world-war-ii-murray-n-rothbard-ed-introduction%3Asiris_sil_1048573 verifies 2002 and scope. Editor's methodological introduction https://cdn.mises.org/ntro%20to%20a%20histroy%20of%20money%20and%20banking_salerno.pdf pp.7–11 and 39 presents governing economic theory as certain and not testable by historical evidence. This protects the causal framework from counterevidence; V1 moderate. The same introduction allows revision of inferred motives, so do not claim all historical facts are immune to evidence. D=7 low-confidence introduction/contents estimate: banking episodes and institutional incentives add learning, doctrinal framing lowers inferential integrity separately. T11 US, T12 finance, endpoint 1945.

Veto V1; confidence moderate. The inspected methodological introduction treats the economic causal laws organizing the historical account as certain and insulated from empirical testing. This forecloses counterevidence to the central explanatory framework. Historical motive judgments can still be revised; the veto is not based simply on Austrian affiliation. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-262330b59677"></a>
### Extraordinary Popular Delusions and the Madness of Crowds — Charles Mackay

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 258.

Counterfactual assessment: popular historical essays, not fiction or devotional advocacy. https://press.uchicago.edu/Misc/Chicago/301259.html traces the influential tulip narrative to poorly checked propaganda and identifies false claims of universal participation and broad economic ruin. The episode materially supports the book's crowd-irrationality argument; V1 moderate, not a claim that every chapter is false. Original 1841, not modern reprint. D=6.5 low-confidence contents/review estimate: varied cases add historical material, colorful anecdote often substitutes for explanation. T12 popular belief/philosophy and financial crowd behavior; T11=0. H13=0 cross-period essays; P14 capped.

Veto V1; confidence moderate. The influential tulipmania case materially supports the crowd-irrationality account using propagated anecdotes and claims of universal participation and broad ruin contradicted by archival scholarship. This is a central evidential failure, not an assertion that every historical essay is false. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

<a id="shelf-5d6e6446dc83"></a>
### Atomic Audit — Stephen I. Schwartz (editor)

Status: **recommended**. Sources: 258, 259.

Reconstructs nuclear weapons costs across production, deployment, command, disposal and oversight. The publisher contents support a system-wide accounting investigation rather than a technical reference manual. Status: **recommended**. Sources: 258.

Sustained explanation of mutation, replication error and the evolution of complex genetic systems; also published as The Cooperative Gene. https://www.nature.com/articles/35086627 confirms title equivalence, 2000 original and exposition through thought experiments rather than specialist mathematics. Not Matt Ridley's work. D=8.5 moderate confidence from Andrew Berry's specialist review and contents: mutation loads, genetic conflict and reproductive mechanisms add distinct constraints; whole text not inspected. Hypotheses about sex and complexity are not treated as universally settled. No ecology/disease deduction, T11/T12/H13=0. References 304–315 in the US edition are documented, not inferred.

<a id="shelf-3bf83d641351"></a>
### Czechoslovakia: The State That Failed — Mary Heimann

Status: **vetoed**. Sources: 258.

V1 moderate confidence: central failure narrative overextends an insufficiently specified nationalism explanation across markedly different regimes, while underweighting contrary democratic evidence. Volker Zimmermann's specialist review https://www.hsozkult.de/publicationreview/id/reb-13850 acknowledges legitimate corrections to heroic national narratives but documents omitted scholarship, uneven coverage of central interwar evidence and one-sided synthesis; Ivo Feierabend in https://files.scriptum.cz/scriptum/kosmas/kosmas_2010_24-1.pdf independently challenges the undefined one-factor causal explanation. Publisher https://www.jstor.org/stable/j.ctt5vkv5s confirms 2009, thesis and endpoint 1992. Veto is for the causal/evidential defect, not criticism of nationalism. D=6.5 moderate confidence from detailed specialist reviews and contents: postwar political detail supplies historical learning despite inferential weakness; do not deduct reliability again under density. Counterfactual retains flaws and both national-history/politics deductions.

Veto V1; confidence moderate. Central causal narrative overextends an undefined nationalist explanation across distinct regimes and underweights contrary democratic evidence, corroborated by two specialist critiques; revisionist conclusions alone are not the veto. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.

### Descartes's Error — Antonio R. Damasio

Status: **recommended**. Sources: 258.

Explanatory argument linking bodily emotion and decision-making, not a clinical curriculum. https://www.loc.gov/item/94028473/ verifies 1994 and references 269–297 of 312 pages. The somatic-marker hypothesis is not treated as settled: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16197997/ identifies ambiguity in psychophysiological interpretation and limited causal evidence. That warrants cautious E, not automatic V1 for an explicitly proposed scientific hypothesis. D=7.5 low confidence from structure and scholarly discussion; lesion cases add distinctions, whole-book pacing unverified. Neurological cases support a general account, so disease does not predominate. Philosophy is framing around neuroscience rather than the central domain; no T12, H13=0.

Eligibility: nonfiction explanatory work, with no established hard exclusion or serious veto in the inspected source report and available bibliographic evidence. Overall and density confidence: **low**; no full-text density audit is claimed.
